\langle f_{i,j}:i\leq j<\lambda^{+}\rangle
such that for all
i<\lambda^{+}
:

(1)

f_{i,j}:N_{i}\rightarrow N_{j}
form a continuous directed system.

(2)

\mathbf{m}_{i}^{0}\smallfrown N_{i}
is an independent
I
-system.

(3)

f_{i,j}
fixes
\mathbf{m}_{i}^{0}
.

(4)

\mathbf{m}_{i+1}^{0}
contains
a_{i}
.

(5)

If
\mathbf{m}_{i}^{0}\smallfrown N_{i}
is
not
reduced in
\mathbf{m}
, then
\mathbf{m}_{i+1}^{0}
,
N_{i+1}
, and
f_{i,i+1}
witness it. That is,
\mathbf{m}_{i}^{0}\smallfrown f_{i,i+1}[N_{i}]\not\leq_{\mathfrak{i}}\mathbf{m}_{i+1}^{0}\smallfrown N_{i+1}
.

This is possible using the extension property and taking direct limits at limits (we use strong continuity to see that the direct limit is still an independent system). This is enough: let
\mathbf{m}_{\lambda^{+}}^{0}
,
f_{i,\lambda^{+}}
,
N_{\lambda^{+}}
be the direct limit of the system. Note that
\mathbf{m}_{\lambda^{+}}^{0}\smallfrown N_{\lambda^{+}}
is an independent
I
-system in
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}
, and since
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}^{\prime},I}
is an AEC, this system can be
\leq_{\mathfrak{i}}
-resolved into independent systems of cardinality
\lambda
. By Lemma
5.3
, this implies that there is a club
C\subseteq\lambda^{+}
such that for any
i\in C
,
\mathbf{m}_{i}^{0}\smallfrown f_{i,\lambda^{+}}[N_{i}]\leq_{\mathfrak{i}}\mathbf{m}_{\lambda^{+}}^{0}\smallfrown N_{\lambda^{+}}
. Now if
i\in C
and
\mathbf{m}_{i}^{0}\smallfrown N_{i}
is
not
reduced in
\mathbf{m}
, then by property (
5
),
\mathbf{m}_{i}^{0}\smallfrown f_{i,i+1}[N_{i}]\not\leq_{\mathfrak{i}}\mathbf{m}_{i+1}^{0}\smallfrown N_{i+1}
. Taking the image of this statement under
f_{i+1,\lambda^{+}}
, we get that
\mathbf{m}_{i}^{0}\smallfrown f_{i,\lambda^{+}}[N_{i}]\not\leq_{\mathfrak{i}}\mathbf{m}_{i+1}^{0}\smallfrown f_{i+1,\lambda^{+}}[N_{i+1}]
. By monotonicity 2, this means that
\mathbf{m}_{i}^{0}\smallfrown f_{i,\lambda^{+}}[N_{i}]\not\leq_{\mathfrak{i}}\mathbf{m}_{i+1}^{0}\smallfrown N_{\lambda^{+}}
, a contradiction. Thus for any
i\in C
,
\mathbf{m}_{i}^{0}\smallfrown N_{i}

is
reduced in
\mathbf{m}
. Let
\langle\alpha_{i}:i<\lambda^{+}\rangle
be a normal enumeration of
C
and let
\mathbf{m}_{i}\coloneqq\mathbf{m}_{\alpha_{i}}^{0}\smallfrown N_{\alpha_{i}}
.
∎

We use reduced systems to build prime ones:

Definition 12.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{i}
be a multidimensional independence relation.

(1)

Let
I=J\cup\{v\}
be in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{i}}
with
J<v
, and let
\mathbf{m}
be an independent
I
-system. We say that
\mathbf{m}
is
prime
if whenever
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}
is an independent
J
-system with
\mathbf{m}\,{\upharpoonright}\,J=\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\,{\upharpoonright}\,J
, there exists
f:\mathbf{m}\rightarrow\mathbf{m}^{\prime}
a systems embedding fixing
\mathbf{m}\,{\upharpoonright}\,J
.

(2)

We say that
\mathfrak{i}
has
prime extension
if for any
I=J\cup\{v\}\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{i}}
with
J<v
and any independent
J
-system
\mathbf{m}
, there is a
prime

I
-system
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbf{m}\cong\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\,{\upharpoonright}\,J
. We define variations such as
(\lambda,n)
-prime extension as in Definition
10.2
.

Lemma 12.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{i}
be a very good multidimensional independence notion, let
\Theta\coloneqq\operatorname{dom}(\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}})
. Let
I=J\cup\{v\}
be in
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{i}}
,
J<v
. Assume that
I
is an initial segment of
\mathcal{P}(n)
. Let
\lambda\in\Theta
be such that
\lambda^{+}\in\Theta
,
\mathfrak{i}
has
(\lambda,n+1)
-extension and
(\lambda,n+1)
-uniqueness. If
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}}
is categorical in
\lambda^{+}
, then any independent
J
-system
\mathbf{m}
in
\mathfrak{i}_{\lambda^{+}}
can be extended to a
prime
independent
I
-system in
\mathfrak{i}_{\lambda^{+}}
.

Proof.

Similar to
[
She09a
, III.4.9]
(or
[
Vas17a
, 3.6]
), using Lemma
12.2
to get the resolution into reduced triples.
∎

Theorem 12.5

(The prime extension theorem)
.

Let
\mathfrak{i}
be a very good multidimensional independence notion, let
\Theta\coloneqq\operatorname{dom}(\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}})
. Let
\lambda\in\Theta
be such that
\lambda^{+}\in\Theta
and
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}}
is categorical in
\lambda^{+}
. If
\mathfrak{i}
has
(\lambda,n)
-extension and
(\lambda,n)
-uniqueness, then
\mathfrak{i}
has
(\lambda^{+},<n)
-prime extension.

Proof.

Immediate from Lemma
12.4
.
∎

13.
Excellent classes

In this section, we introduce the definition of an
excellent
AEC (this generalizes the definition in
[
She83a
,
She83b
]
). We show how to obtain excellent AECs from the setups considered earlier (Lemma
13.3
) and explain why excellent classes are tame and have primes (Theorem
13.9
), hence (as we will see in the next section) admit categoricity transfers. We also show how to get excellence in compact AECs (Theorem
13.7
) and in
(<\omega)
-extendible good frames, using the weak diamond (Theorem
13.6
).

Definition 13.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{i}
be a multidimensional independence relation. We call
\mathfrak{i}

excellent
if:

(1)

\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}}
is an AEC.

(2)

\mathfrak{i}
is very good.

(3)

\mathfrak{i}
has extension and uniqueness.

We say that an AEC
\mathfrak{K}
is
excellent
if there exists an excellent multidimensional independence relation
\mathfrak{i}
such that
\mathfrak{K}=\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}}
.

Reasonable multidimensional independence relations will be tame:

Lemma 13.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{i}
be a multidimensional independence relation. If
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}}
is an AEC,
\mathfrak{i}
has extension and uniqueness, and
\mathfrak{i}
satisfies the definition of being very good, except possibly for
\mathfrak{s}(\mathop{\hbox{\lower 2.0pt\hbox to12.77773pt{\hfil\vrule width=0.3pt,depth=-2.0pt,height=10.0pt\hfil}\lower 2.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\smile$}}}\limits(\mathfrak{i}))
being a good frame, then
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}}
is
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}})
-tame.

Proof.

As in
[
She09a
, III.1.10]
(see also
[
GK
]
).
∎

The next two lemmas tell us how to derive excellence from the conclusion of the limit excellence theorem (Theorem
11.17
).

Lemma 13.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{i}
be a very good multidimensional independence relation. Let
\lambda\coloneqq\min(\operatorname{dom}(\mathfrak{i}))
. If
\mathfrak{i}
has
(\lambda,<\omega)
-extension and
(\lambda,<\omega)
-uniqueness, then there exists a unique excellent multidimensional independence relation
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}
such that
(\mathfrak{i}^{\prime})_{\lambda}=\mathfrak{i}_{\lambda}
. In particular, the AEC generated by
(\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\lambda}
is excellent.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be the AEC generated by
(\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\lambda}
. Let
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}
be as described by Lemma
11.5
. It satisfies all the conditions in the definition of being very good, except perhaps for existence, uniqueness, and
\mathfrak{s}(\mathop{\hbox{\lower 2.0pt\hbox to12.77773pt{\hfil\vrule width=0.3pt,depth=-2.0pt,height=10.0pt\hfil}\lower 2.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\smile$}}}\limits(\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}))
being a good frame. By Lemma
11.6
,
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}
has extension and uniqueness. By Lemma
13.2
,
\mathfrak{K}
is
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
-tame. Since
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}
has extension,
\mathfrak{K}
has in particular amalgamation. By Fact
6.11
, there is a good frame
\mathfrak{s}
with underlying class
\mathfrak{K}
. Let
\mathfrak{t}\coloneqq\mathfrak{s}(\mathop{\hbox{\lower 2.0pt\hbox to12.77773pt{\hfil\vrule width=0.3pt,depth=-2.0pt,height=10.0pt\hfil}\lower 2.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\smile$}}}\limits(\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}))
. It is clear from the definition that
\mathfrak{s}_{\lambda}=\mathfrak{t}_{\lambda}
. Moreover,
\mathfrak{t}
satisfies most of the axioms of a good frame (Fact
7.5
). The only axiom that can fail is local character. However since
\mathop{\hbox{\lower 2.0pt\hbox to12.77773pt{\hfil\vrule width=0.3pt,depth=-2.0pt,height=10.0pt\hfil}\lower 2.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\smile$}}}\limits(\mathfrak{i}^{\prime})
is very good, it has local character, so every type does not
\mathfrak{t}
-fork over a model of size
\lambda
. By Lemma
6.5
and Remark
6.6
, this implies that
\mathfrak{s}=\mathfrak{t}
, so
\mathfrak{t}
is a good frame. Thus
\mathfrak{i}^{\prime}
is very good, and hence excellent.
∎

Lemma 13.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{i}
be a very good multidimensional independence notion and let
\Theta\coloneqq\operatorname{dom}(\mathfrak{i})
. Let
\lambda\coloneqq\min(\Theta)
and let
\mathfrak{K}
be the AEC generated by
\left(\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)_{\lambda}
. If:

(A)

\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
\lambda
.

(B)

\mathfrak{i}^{\text{brim}}
has
(\lambda,<\omega)
-existence and
(\lambda,<\omega)
-uniqueness.

Then
\mathfrak{K}
is excellent.

Proof.

By Lemma
11.10
,
\mathfrak{i}^{\ast}
also has strong
(\lambda,<\omega)
-uniqueness. By Theorems
9.12
and
9.15
, we obtain that
\mathfrak{i}
also has
(\lambda,<\omega)
-extension and
(\lambda,<\omega)
-uniqueness. Now apply Lemma
13.3
.
∎

Assuming a weak version of the generalized continuum hypothesis, we obtain excellence in
(<\omega)
-extendible very good frames. We will use the following notation:

Definition 13.5
.

For
S
a class of cardinals, we write
\operatorname{WGCH}(S)
for the statement “
2^{\lambda}<2^{\lambda^{+}}
for all
\lambda\in S
”. We write
\operatorname{WGCH}
instead of
\operatorname{WGCH}(\operatorname{Card})
, where
Card
is the class of all cardinals.

Theorem 13.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}
be a
(<\omega)
-extendible categorical very good
\lambda
-frame. Let
\mathfrak{K}
be the AEC generated by
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{s}}
. If
\operatorname{WGCH}([\lambda,\lambda^{+\omega}))
holds, then
\mathfrak{K}
is excellent.

Proof.

Let
\Theta\coloneqq[\lambda,\lambda^{+\omega})
. By Fact
7.20
(
4
), there is a very good categorical frame
\mathfrak{t}
such that
\operatorname{dom}(\mathfrak{t})=\Theta
and
\mathfrak{t}_{\lambda^{+n}}=\mathfrak{s}^{+n}
for all
n<\omega
. By definition of being very good, there is a very good two-dimensional independence notion
\mathop{\hbox{\lower 2.0pt\hbox to12.77773pt{\hfil\vrule width=0.3pt,depth=-2.0pt,height=10.0pt\hfil}\lower 2.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\smile$}}}\limits
on
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{t}}
.

Fix
\mathfrak{i}
and
\mathfrak{i}^{\ast}
satisfying the conclusion of Theorem
11.3
. By Lemma
11.17
, we have that
\mathfrak{i}^{\ast}
has
(\lambda,<\omega)
-existence and
(\lambda,<\omega)
-uniqueness. Now apply Lemma
13.4
.
∎

In a compact AEC, excellence follows from categoricity (without any cardinal arithmetic hypothesis)

Theorem 13.7
.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be a compact AEC. If
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in some
\mu>\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
, then
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+6}\text{-sat}}
is an excellent AEC.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.8
,
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in a proper class of cardinals, so we might as well assume that
\mu\geq\beth_{\omega}(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}))
. Fix
\mathfrak{i}
satisfying the conclusion of Theorem
11.13
. Let
\Theta\coloneqq[\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+6},\beth_{\omega}(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})))
. By Lemma
11.17
, we have that
\mathfrak{i}^{\text{brim}}
has
(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+6},<\omega)
-existence and
(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+6},<\omega)
-uniqueness. The result now follows from Lemma
13.4
.
∎

An appropriate subclass of saturated models of an excellent class will always have primes in the following sense:

Definition 13.8

(
[
She09a
, III.3.2]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be an abstract class.

(1)

A
prime triple
is a triple
(a,M,N)
such that
M\leq_{\mathfrak{K}}N
,
a\in|N|
, and whenever
\mathbf{tp}(b/M;N^{\prime})=\mathbf{tp}(a/M;N)
, there exists a
\mathfrak{K}
-embedding
f:N\xrightarrow[M]{}N^{\prime}
such that
f(b)=a
.

(2)

\mathfrak{K}

has primes
if for any
p\in\mathscr{S}(M)
there exists a prime triple
(a,M,N)
such that
p=\mathbf{tp}(a/M;N)
.

Theorem 13.9

(Structure of excellent AECs)
.

If
\mathfrak{K}
is an excellent AEC, then
\mathfrak{K}
is not empty, has amalgamation, joint embedding, no maximal models and is
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
-tame. Moreover,
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}\text{-sat}}
has primes.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\coloneqq\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
. Let
\mathfrak{i}
be an excellent multidimensional independence relation with
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{i}}=\mathfrak{K}
.
\mathfrak{K}
is not empty and has no maximal models because
\mathfrak{i}
has
1
-extension, and
\mathfrak{K}
has amalgamation and joint embedding because
\mathfrak{i}
has
1
-uniqueness (see Lemma
10.5
). By Lemma
13.2
,
\mathfrak{K}
is
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
-tame. Let
\mathfrak{i}^{\ast}
be the restriction of
\mathfrak{i}
to the fragmented AEC
\mathfrak{K}^{\ast}
such that
\mathfrak{K}^{\ast}_{\leq\lambda}=\mathfrak{K}_{\lambda}
and
\mathfrak{K}^{\ast}_{>\lambda}={\mathfrak{K}}^{\lambda^{+}\text{-sat}}
. It is easy to check that
\mathfrak{i}^{\ast}
is still a very good multidimensional independence relation with extension and uniqueness. By Theorem
12.5
,
\mathfrak{i}^{\ast}
has
(\lambda^{+},<\omega)
-prime extension. By standard arguments similar to those in
[
She83b
, §5]
, one gets that
\mathfrak{i}^{\ast}_{\geq\lambda^{+}}
has prime extension.

Now by
[
She09a
, III.4.9]
(see also
[
Vas17a
, 3.6]
),
\mathfrak{K}^{\ast}_{\lambda^{+}}
has primes. It remains to check that
\mathfrak{K}^{\ast}_{>\lambda^{+}}
has primes. Let
M\in\mathfrak{K}^{\ast}_{>\lambda^{+}}
and let
p\in\mathscr{S}(M)
. Let
N\in\mathfrak{K}^{\ast}
be such that
M\leq_{\mathfrak{K}}N
and
N
realizes
p
, say with
a
. By local character for
\mathop{\hbox{\lower 2.0pt\hbox to12.77773pt{\hfil\vrule width=0.3pt,depth=-2.0pt,height=10.0pt\hfil}\lower 2.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\smile$}}}\limits\coloneqq\mathop{\hbox{\lower 2.0pt\hbox to12.77773pt{\hfil\vrule width=0.3pt,depth=-2.0pt,height=10.0pt\hfil}\lower 2.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\smile$}}}\limits(\mathfrak{i}^{\ast})
, there exists
M_{0},N_{0}\in\mathfrak{K}_{\lambda}^{\ast}
such that
a\in|N_{0}|
and
M\mathop{\hbox{\lower 2.0pt\hbox to12.77773pt{\hfil\vrule width=0.3pt,depth=-2.0pt,height=10.0pt\hfil}\lower 2.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\smile$}}}\limits_{M_{0}}^{N}N_{0}
. Since
\mathfrak{K}_{\lambda^{+}}^{\ast}
has primes, we may pick
N_{0}^{\ast}\leq_{\mathfrak{K}}N_{0}
such that
(a,M_{0},N_{0})
is a prime triple in
\mathfrak{K}^{\ast}
. By monotonicity, we still have
M\mathop{\hbox{\lower 2.0pt\hbox to12.77773pt{\hfil\vrule width=0.3pt,depth=-2.0pt,height=10.0pt\hfil}\lower 2.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\smile$}}}\limits_{M_{0}}^{N}N_{0}^{\ast}
, so by prime extension (applied to the independent system
\mathbf{m}
consisting of
M_{0}
,
M
, and
N_{0}^{\ast}
), there exists a prime model
N^{\ast}
over
\mathbf{m}
, which can be taken to be contained inside
N
. Now check that
(a,M,N^{\ast})
is a prime triple.
∎

14.
Excellence and categoricity: the main theorems

In this section, we combine the results derived so far about excellence with known categoricity transfers to obtain the main theorems of this paper. In addition to Facts
3.25
,
3.26
, the facts we will use about categoricity are:

Fact 14.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be an
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
-tame AEC with amalgamation, and arbitrarily large models. Assume that
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
some

\mu>\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
. Then:

(1)

[
Vas17b
, 10.9]
14
14

14

The main ideas of the proof appear in
[
Vas17d
,
Vas18
]
.
If
\mathfrak{K}
has primes then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in all
\mu^{\prime}\geq\mu
.

(2)

[
Vas17b
, 10.3,10.4]
15
15

15

The upward part of the transfer (i.e. getting categoricity in
\mu^{\prime}\geq\mu
) is due to Grossberg and VanDieren
[
GV06c
,
GV06a
]
.
If
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
and
\mu
is a successor, then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
all

\mu^{\prime}\geq\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
.

We obtain the following categoricity transfer for excellent classes (recall Definition
2.1
):

Theorem 14.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be an excellent AEC. If
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
some

\mu>\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
, then there exists
\chi<h(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}))
such that
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
all

\mu^{\prime}\geq\min(\mu,\chi)
. If in addition
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
, then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in all
\mu^{\prime}>\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
.

Proof.

By Fact
3.26
, we may assume that
\mu>\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}
. By Fact
14.1
and Theorem
13.9
,
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}\text{-sat}}
is categorical on a tail of cardinals. By Fact
3.25
,
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in all
\mu^{\prime}\geq\min(\mu,\chi)
. If
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
, then it will be categorical in a successor, hence by Fact
14.1
again,
\mathfrak{K}
will be categorical in all cardinals above
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
.
∎

When working inside a
(<\omega)
-extendible categorical good
\lambda
-frame, it is known how to transfer categoricity across finite successors of
\lambda
, so we can be more precise:

Lemma 14.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}
be a
(<\omega)
-extendible categorical good
\lambda
-frame. Let
\mathfrak{K}
be the AEC generated by
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{s}}
. If there exists
n<\omega
such that the AEC generated by
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{s}^{+n}}
is excellent, then:

(1)

\mathfrak{K}
has arbitrarily large models.

(2)

If in addition
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
some

\mu>\lambda
, then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
all

\mu^{\prime}>\lambda
,
\mathfrak{K}_{\geq\mu}
has amalgamation, no maximal models, and is
\mu
-tame.

Proof.

Fix
n<\omega
such that the AEC
\mathfrak{K}^{*}
generated by
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{s}^{+n}}
is excellent.

Let

(\ast)_{1}

\mathfrak{K}_{\ell}
be the AEC generated by
\mathfrak{s}^{+\ell}
, so
\mathfrak{K}_{\ell}
is categorical in
\lambda^{+\ell}.

Now,

(\ast)_{2}

\boxplus

(a)

if
\ell<\omega,
then
\mathfrak{K}_{\ell}
is categorical in
\lambda^{\ell+1}
iff
\mathfrak{K}_{\ell}
is categorical in
\lambda^{\ell+2},

(b)

if
0<\ell<n
then
\mathfrak{K}_{\ell-1}
is categorical in
\lambda^{+\ell+1}
iff
\mathfrak{K}_{\ell}
is categorical in
\lambda^{+\ell+1}
iff
M\in\mathfrak{K}_{\lambda^{+\ell+1}}\Rightarrow M\in\mathfrak{K}_{\ell-1}\cong M\in\mathfrak{K}_{\ell}.

[Why? By
[
Vas17b
, Theorem A.9]
, here the doubts about its use in
6.9
do not arise.]

Hence,

(\ast)

either for all
\ell<k<\omega,

\mathfrak{K}_{\ell}
is categorical in
\lambda^{+1}

or
for all
\ell<k<\omega,

K_{\ell}
is not categorical in
\lambda^{+k}.

So if the first possibility in
(\ast)
holds, by
14.2
we are done. So we can assume the second one in
(\ast)
holds, hence necessarily
\mu\geq\lambda^{+\omega}.
Still
\mathfrak{K}_{\ast}=\mathfrak{K}_{n}
is categorical in
\lambda^{+n},
hence by
14.2
it is categorical in
\lambda^{n+n+1}
but this contradicts “the second case in
(\ast)
holds”, so we are done.
∎

Thus assuming some instances of the weak diamond, we obtain the following eventual categoricity transfer for
(<\omega)
-extendible frames:

Corollary 14.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}
be a
(<\omega)
-extendible categorical good
\lambda
-frame. Let
\mathfrak{K}
be the AEC generated by
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{s}}
. Assume
\operatorname{WGCH}([\lambda^{+n},\lambda^{+\omega}))
holds for some
n<\omega
. Then
\mathfrak{K}
has arbitrarily large models and if
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
some

\mu>\lambda
, then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
all

\mu^{\prime}>\lambda
and moreover
\mathfrak{K}_{\geq\mu}
has amalgamation, no maximal models, and is
\mu
-tame.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{t}\coloneqq\mathfrak{s}^{+(n+3)}
. By Fact
7.20
(
2
),
\mathfrak{t}
is very good. By Theorem
13.6
, the AEC
\mathfrak{K}^{\ast}
generated by
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathfrak{t}}
is excellent. Now apply Lemma
14.3
.
∎

Specializing to compact AECs, we get:

Theorem 14.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be a compact AEC. Let
\mu>\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
. If
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
\mu
, then there exists
\chi<h(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}))
such that
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in all
\mu^{\prime}\geq\min(\mu,\chi)
.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.8
,
\mathfrak{K}
has amalgamation, no maximal models, and is categorical in a proper class of cardinals. We will also use without comments Fact
3.24
, which says in particular that
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\lambda\text{-sat}}
is an AEC for any
\lambda>\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
. By Theorem
13.7
,
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+6}\text{-sat}}
is excellent. By Theorem
14.2
,
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+6}\text{-sat}}
is categorical in all high-enough cardinals. Since
\mathfrak{K}
itself is categorical in a proper class of cardinals, this implies that
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}\text{-sat}}
is also categorical in all high-enough cardinals, and so in particular in a high-enough successor cardinal. Now
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}\text{-sat}}
is categorical in
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}=\text{LS}({\mathfrak{K}}^{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}\text{-sat}})
, and hence by Fact
14.1
it is categorical in
all

\mu^{\prime}\geq\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}
. Now apply Morley’s omitting type theorem for AECs (Fact
3.25
) to get the desired conclusion about categoricity in
\mathfrak{K}
.
∎

Corollary 14.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be a compact AEC. If
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
some

\mu\geq h(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}))
, then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
all

\mu^{\prime}\geq h(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}))
.

Proof.

This is a special case of Theorem
14.5
.
∎

Corollary 14.7
.

(1)

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be an AEC and let
\kappa>\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
be a strongly compact cardinal. If
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
some

\mu\geq h(\kappa)
, then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
all

\mu^{\prime}\geq h(\kappa)
.

(2)

Let
T
be a theory in
\mathbb{L}_{\kappa,\omega}
,
\kappa
a strongly compact cardinal. If
T
is categorical in
some

\mu\geq h(|T|+|\tau(T)|+\kappa)
, then
T
is categorical in
all

\mu^{\prime}\geq h(|T|+|\tau(T)|+\kappa)
.

(3)

Let
\psi
be an
\mathbb{L}_{\kappa,\omega}
sentence
\kappa
-strongly compact. If
\psi
is categorical in
some

\mu\geq h(\kappa)
, then
\psi
is categorical in
all

\lambda^{\prime}\geq h(\kappa)
.

Proof.

The first two parts are by Fact
4.3
and Corollary
14.6
. The third part follows from the second because an
\mathbb{L}_{\kappa,\omega}
sentence has a vocabulary of size strictly less than
\kappa
.
∎

Regarding AECs with amalgamation, the following lemma says that (assuming WGCH) with a little bit of tameness, we can transfer categoricity. Note that the proof only uses amalgamation below the categoricity cardinal, but transfers it above.

Lemma 14.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be an AEC with arbitrarily large models. Assume
\operatorname{WGCH}([\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}),\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+\omega}))
. Let
\mu>\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}
and assume that
\mathfrak{K}_{<\max(\mu,\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+\omega})}
has amalgamation and no maximal models. If
\mathfrak{K}^{\text{sat}}_{(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}),\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+\omega})}
is
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
-tame and
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
\mu
, then there is
\chi<h(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}))
such that
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in all
\mu^{\prime}\geq\min(\mu,\chi)
.

Proof.

We use without further comments (Fact
3.24
) that for any
\lambda\in(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}),\mu]
,
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\lambda\text{-sat}}
is an AEC with Löwenheim-Skolem-Tarski number
\lambda
. In particular, the model of cardinality
\mu
is saturated. By Fact
6.2
, there is a (categorical) good frame
\mathfrak{s}
on
\mathfrak{K}^{\text{sat}}_{[\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+},\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+\omega})}
. By Fact
7.16
,
\mathfrak{s}
is
(<\omega)
-extendible. By Corollary
14.4
,
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}\text{-sat}}
is categorical in all
\mu^{\prime}\geq\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}
and has amalgamation above
\mu
. In particular,
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in all
\mu^{\prime}\geq\mu
and
\mathfrak{K}_{\geq\mu}
has amalgamation. Since we knew that
\mathfrak{K}_{<\mu}
had amalgamation, we have that
\mathfrak{K}
has amalgamation. By Morley’s omitting type theorem for AECs (Fact
3.25
), there is
\chi<h(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}))
such that
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in all
\mu^{\prime}\geq\min(\mu,\chi)
.
∎

Since tameness can be derived from high-enough categoricity, we obtain:

Theorem 14.9
.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be an AEC with arbitrarily large models. Let
\mu\geq\aleph_{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}}
. Assume there exists unboundedly-many
\lambda<\aleph_{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}}
such that
\operatorname{WGCH}([\lambda,\lambda^{+\omega}))
holds. If
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
\mu
and
\mathfrak{K}_{<\mu}
has amalgamation and no maximal models, then there exists
\chi<\aleph_{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}}
such that
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in all
\mu^{\prime}\geq\min(h(\chi),\mu)
.

Proof.

By Fact
3.24
(
2
) (where
\lambda
there stands for
\aleph_{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}}
here), there is
\chi_{0}<\aleph_{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}}
such that
\mathfrak{K}^{\text{sat}}_{(\chi_{0},\aleph_{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}})}
is
\chi_{0}
-tame. By Lemma
14.8
applied to
\mathfrak{K}_{\geq\chi_{0}}
, we get the result.
∎

Corollary 14.10
.

Assume
\operatorname{WGCH}
(see Definition
13.5
). Let
\mathfrak{K}
be an AEC with amalgamation. If
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
some

\mu\geq h(\aleph_{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}})
, then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
all

\mu^{\prime}\geq h(\aleph_{\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})^{+}})
.

Proof.

This is a special case of Theorem
14.9
. It is well-known that AECs with a model in
h(\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K}))
have arbitrarily large models. Moreover, by considering the equivalence relation “
M
and
N
embed into a common model”, we can partition the AEC into disjoint classes, each of which have joint embedding. One can then work inside the unique class with arbitrarily large models, which will have joint embedding and no maximal models. It is then easy to see that the original class must also be categorical in all high-enough cardinals. See for example
[
Vas16a
, 10.13]
for the details of this argument.
∎

Corollary 14.11
.

Assume
2^{\theta}<2^{\theta^{+}}
for all cardinals
\theta.
Let
\mathfrak{K}
be an AEC and let
\kappa>\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
be a measureable cardinal. If
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in some
\mu\geq\beth_{(2^{\aleph_{\kappa^{+}}})+},
then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
all
\mu^{\prime}\geq\beth_{(2^{\aleph_{\kappa^{+}}})+}.

Proof.

By the main theorem of
[
KS
]
,
\mathfrak{K}_{[\kappa,\mu)}
has amalgamation and
\mathfrak{K}_{\geq\kappa}
has no maximal models. Apply Theorem
14.9
.
∎

We can also obtain results from completely local hypotheses about the number of models in the
\lambda^{+n}
’s. This was already stated in
[
She09a
, III.12.43]
. Below,
\mathbb{I}(\mathfrak{K},\lambda)
denotes the number of models (up to isomorphism) in
\mathfrak{K}_{\lambda}
. On
\mu_{\text{unif}}
, see
[
She09b
, VII.0.4]
for the definition and
[
She09b
, VII.9.4]
on what is known (morally,
\mu_{\text{unif}}(\lambda^{+},2^{\lambda})=2^{\lambda^{+}}
when
2^{\lambda}<2^{\lambda^{+}}
).

Remark 14.12
.

Recall for comparison
[
She09d
, 7.12]

(\boxplus)

For some cardinal
\lambda_{\ast}<\chi
and a cardinal
\lambda_{\ast\ast}<\beth_{1,1}(\lambda_{\ast}^{+\omega})
above
\lambda_{\ast},\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in every cardinal
\lambda\geq\lambda_{\ast\ast}
but in no
\lambda\in(\lambda_{\ast},\lambda_{\ast\ast})
provided that:

\circledast_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mu,\chi}

(a)

K
is an a.e.c. categorical in
\mu,

(b)

\mathfrak{K}
has amalgamation and
\rm{JEP}
in every
\lambda<\aleph_{\chi},\lambda\geq\rm{LS}(\mathfrak{K}),

(c)

\chi
is a limit cardinal,
\text{cf}(\chi)>\rm{LS}(\mathfrak{K}),
and for arbitrarily large
\lambda<\chi
the sequence
\langle 2^{\lambda^{+n}}:n<\omega\rangle
is increasing,

(d)

\mu>\beth_{1,1}(\lambda)
for every
\lambda<\chi
hence
\mu\geq\aleph_{\chi},

(e)

every
M\in K_{<\aleph_{\chi}}
has a
\leq_{\mathfrak{K}}
-extension in
K_{\mu}.

Theorem 14.13
.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be an AEC and let
\lambda\geq\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})
be such that
\operatorname{WGCH}([\lambda,\lambda^{+\omega)})
holds. Assume that
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
\lambda
,
\lambda^{+}
,
\mathfrak{K}_{\lambda^{++}}\neq\emptyset
, and
\mathbb{I}(\mathfrak{K},\lambda^{+n})<\mu_{\text{unif}}(\lambda^{+n},2^{\lambda^{+(n-1)}})
for all
n\in[2,\omega)
. Then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in all
\mu>\lambda
.

Proof.

As in the proof of
[
She09a
, II.9.2]
, we get that there is an
\omega
-successful
\text{good}^{+}

\lambda
-frame
\mathfrak{s}
on
\mathfrak{K}_{\lambda}
. This implies (Fact
7.20
(
1
)) that
\mathfrak{s}
is
(<\omega)
-extendible. Now apply Lemma
14.3
.
∎

When
\lambda=\aleph_{0}
, the hypotheses can be weakened and we obtain the following generalization of the main result of
[
She83a
,
She83b
]
:

Theorem 14.14
.

Let
\mathfrak{K}
be a
\operatorname{PC}_{\aleph_{0}}
AEC (so
\text{LS}(\mathfrak{K})=\aleph_{0}
). Assume
\operatorname{WGCH}([\aleph_{0},\aleph_{\omega}))
. Assume that
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
\aleph_{0}
,
1\leq\mathbb{I}(\mathfrak{K},\aleph_{1})<2^{\aleph_{1}}
, and
\mathbb{I}(\mathfrak{K},\aleph_{n})<\mu_{\text{unif}}(\aleph_{n},2^{\aleph_{n-1}})
for all
n\in[2,\omega)
. Then
\mathfrak{K}
has arbitrarily large models. Moreover, if
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
some
uncountable cardinal, then
\mathfrak{K}
is categorical in
all
uncountable cardinals.

Proof.

As in the proof of Theorem
14.13
, using
[
She09a
, II.9.3]
instead of
[
She09a
, II.9.2]
.
∎
